<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm"></a>

# Source Code Management – Apache Whisker::Velocity

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm--Overview"></a>

# Overview

This project uses [Git](https://git-scm.com/) to manage its source code. Instructions on Git use can be found at [https://git-scm.com/doc](https://git-scm.com/doc).

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm--Web_Browser_Access"></a>

# Web Browser Access

The following is a link to a browsable version of the source repository:

```text
https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf?p=creadur-whisker.git/apache-whisker-velocity
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm--Anonymous_Access"></a>

# Anonymous Access

The source can be checked out anonymously from Git with this command (See [https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone)):

```bash
$ git clone https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf/creadur-whisker.git
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm--Developer_Access"></a>

# Developer Access

Only project developers can access the Git tree via this method (See [https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone)).

```bash
$ git clone https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf/creadur-whisker.git
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-scm--Access_from_Behind_a_Firewall"></a>

# Access from Behind a Firewall

Refer to the documentation of the SCM used for more information about access behind a firewall.

# Project Team – Apache Whisker::Velocity

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-velocity-team--Project_Team"></a>

# Project Team

A successful project requires many people to play many roles. Some members write code or documentation, while others are valuable as testers, submitting patches and suggestions.

The project team is comprised of Members and Contributors. Members have direct access to the source of a project and actively evolve the code-base. Contributors improve the project through submission of patches and suggestions to the Members. The number of Contributors to the project is unbounded. Get involved today. All contributions to the project are greatly appreciated.

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm"></a>

# Source Code Management – Apache Whisker::XML

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm--Overview"></a>

# Overview

This project uses [Git](https://git-scm.com/) to manage its source code. Instructions on Git use can be found at [https://git-scm.com/doc](https://git-scm.com/doc).

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm--Web_Browser_Access"></a>

# Web Browser Access

The following is a link to a browsable version of the source repository:

```text
https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf?p=creadur-whisker.git/apache-whisker-xml
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm--Anonymous_Access"></a>

# Anonymous Access

The source can be checked out anonymously from Git with this command (See [https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone)):

```bash
$ git clone https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf/creadur-whisker.git
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm--Developer_Access"></a>

# Developer Access

Only project developers can access the Git tree via this method (See [https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-clone)).

```bash
$ git clone https://gitbox.apache.org/repos/asf/creadur-whisker.git
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-scm--Access_from_Behind_a_Firewall"></a>

# Access from Behind a Firewall

Refer to the documentation of the SCM used for more information about access behind a firewall.

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-team"></a>

# Project Team – Apache Whisker::XML

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-team--Project_Team"></a>

# Project Team

A successful project requires many people to play many roles. Some members write code or documentation, while others are valuable as testers, submitting patches and suggestions.

The project team is comprised of Members and Contributors. Members have direct access to the source of a project and actively evolve the code-base. Contributors improve the project through submission of patches and suggestions to the Members. The number of Contributors to the project is unbounded. Get involved today. All contributions to the project are greatly appreciated.

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-xml-team--Members"></a>

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index"></a>

# Whisker In 5 Minutes – Apache Whisker&trade;

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Whisker_In_5_Minutes"></a>

# Whisker In 5 Minutes

[ Apache Whisker](https://creadur.apache.org/whisker) is based around [meta-data](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-meta-data) describing the licensing qualities of documents included within [complex software distributions](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--complex-software-applications). Whisker just helps to maintain information more efficiently, and is [no substitute](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-whisker-is-not) for solid legal work. Learn about copyright and software licensing [here](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--more-resources.27).

For this example, we'll create meta-data for a simple [MIT licensed](http://opensource.org/licenses/mit-license.php/) project. This illustrates any project where a NOTICE is not (necessarily) [required](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--when-notice-needed).

If this isn't quite what you're looking for, take a look at other examples which

- add third party resources licensed by
  - an [individual](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-3rd-party-individual)
  - a for- or non-profit [corporation](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-3rd-party-corporate)
  - an [informal group](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-3rd-party-group)
- [parameterising a template](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-license-family) for a [license family](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-is-license-family), featuring the [BSD (2 Clause) license](http://opensource.org/licenses/BSD-2-Clause)
- All about [copyright notices](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-copyright-notices)

or dive into sample meta-data:

- [ Apache James](./james.xml)
- [ Public Domain](./public-domain.xml)
- [ Template License](./template-license.xml)
- [License Family](./license-family-sample.xml)
- distributing work licensed from
  - an [ individual maintainer](./3rd-party-individual-sample.xml)
  - a for- or non-profit [corporation](./3rd-party-corporate-sample.xml)
  - an informal [group](./3rd-party-group-sample.xml)
- [copyright notices](./copyright-notices-sample.xml)

If you're using an IDE then see the [meta data reference](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-meta-data) for a [DTD](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-dtd).

If you have any questions, please try the [FAQ](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq) before asking on the [mail lists](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-mailing-lists).

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Basic_Structure"></a>

## Basic Structure

Each license, notice and organisation is described once above, and then [referenced by id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids) below. Start with the outline.

```xml
<manifest>
    <!-- Definitions -->
    <licenses/>
    <notices/>
    <organisations/>
    <primary-license/>
    <primary-notice/>
    <primary-organisation/>

    <!-- Contents -->
    <within dir='.'/>
</manifest>
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Primary_Definitions"></a>

## Primary Definitions

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Add_The_Primary_License"></a>

### Add The Primary License

[Licenses](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-is-a-license) should be entered once into the `licenses` section (at the top), and then [referenced by id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids).

This project is MIT licensed, so a description of this license needs to be added into `licenses`. Choose a `name` suitable for [display](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--name-freedom), [pick an id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--id-freedom) and give an [url](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--url-freedom). Note the `id` chosen since this will be used a little later. The MIT license isn't a [family](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-is-license-family), so the `text` contains just the plain license text (without [parameters](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-meta-data--Templates_For_License_Families)). We end up with something like

```xml
<manifest>
    <licenses>
        <license
            name='MIT License'
            id='osi:mit'
            url='https://www.opensource.org/licenses/mit-license.php'>
            <text>
Permission is hereby granted, free  of charge, to any person obtaining
a  copy  of this  software  and  associated  documentation files  (the
"Software"), to  deal in  the Software without  restriction, including
without limitation  the rights to  use, copy, modify,  merge, publish,
distribute,  sublicense, and/or sell  copies of  the Software,  and to
permit persons to whom the Software  is furnished to do so, subject to
the following conditions:

The  above  copyright  notice  and  this permission  notice  shall  be
included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE  SOFTWARE IS  PROVIDED  "AS  IS", WITHOUT  WARRANTY  OF ANY  KIND,
EXPRESS OR  IMPLIED, INCLUDING  BUT NOT LIMITED  TO THE  WARRANTIES OF
MERCHANTABILITY,    FITNESS    FOR    A   PARTICULAR    PURPOSE    AND
NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE
LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE,  ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION
WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.
            </text>
        </license>
    </licenses>
    ...
```

By primary license we [mean](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-is-primary) the main license for the project, covering most of the original resources contributed to the distribution by the project. Reference this [by id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids) in `primary-license`. For our example, we set the `id` attribute to the id - `osi:mit` - we picked earlier. Conventionally, a [copyright notice](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-copyright-notice) is positioned above the MIT license text. So we need to add a [primary copyright notice](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-primary-copyright-notice) (*Copyright (c) 3535 Someone*, let's say).

```xml
<manifest>
    ...
    <primary-license id='osi:mit'>
        <copyright-notice>Copyright (c) 3535 Someone</copyright-notice>
    </primary-license>
    ...
</manifest>
```

Unlike the [Apache License, Version 2](https://opensource.org/licenses/apache-2.0) (for example), the MIT License does not have a `NOTICE` [retention clause](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--when-notice-needed). So, there is no need for a `primary-notice` to be defined.

```xml
<manifest>
    ...
    <notices/>
    ...
    <primary-notice/>
    ...
</manifest>
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Add_The_Primary_Organisation"></a>

### Add The Primary Organisation

[Individual maintainers](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--third-party-individual), [informal groups](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--third-party-ad-hoc), for- and non-profit [corporations](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--third-party-corporation) are all modeled by Whisker as `organisations`. Read more [here](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--what-is-org). Let's assume that our example project is maintained by - and licensed from - the (hypothetical) *Hypothetical Example Foundation*, whose domain is `example.org`.

This `organisation` needs to be added into `organisations`. We fill in [`name`](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--name-freedom) and [`url`](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--url-freedom), and [pick an id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--id-freedom) (which we'll need to [refer to](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids) just a little later).

```text
    ...
    <organisations>
        <organisation
            id='example.org'
            name='The Hypothetical Example Foundation'
            url='https://www.example.org'/>
    </organisations>
    ...
```

Find the `primary-organisation` lower in the document, and set the `id` attribute.

```text
    ...
    <primary-organisation id='example.org'/>
    ...
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--Contents"></a>

## Contents

Meta-data links the contents of a software distribution with its licensing definitions. This allows Whisker to, for example, generate licensing document and check that every document included is expected.

Contents are [described by directory](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--how-contents). The resources `within` each directory are grouped `with-license` then `by-organisation`. Directories are modeled by a [flat list](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--how-dir-model) of `within` elements.

Work through the distribution, one directory at a time. Let's say our distribution is laid out like this:

```text
  --|- one.txt
    |- two.html
    |- three.py
    |
    |-alpha-|- five.js
    |       |- six.rb
    |       |- seven.coffee
    |
    |-beta--|- eight.java
```

The root directory of the distribution is [denoted by '.'](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--how-root-dir), and let's say that its contents (`one.txt`, `two.html` and `three.py`) are all licensed from the primary organisation (`example.org`) using the primary license (MIT). Whisker makes no assumptions about license or organisation, whether primary or not. Every resource is added `by-organisation` and `with-license`, referred to [by id](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids).

```text
    ...
    <within dir='.'>
        <with-license id='osi:mit'>
            <by-organisation id='example.org'>
                <resource name='one.text'/>
                <resource name='two.html'/>
                <resource name='three.py'/>
            </by-organisation>
        </with-license>
    </within>
</manifest>
```

In directory `alpha`, let's say that `five.js` and `six.rb` are similar (MIT licensed by `example.org`) to those above. We add these immediately.

```text
    ...
    <within dir='alpha'>
        <with-license id='osi:mit'>
            <by-organisation id='example.org'>
                <resource name='five.js'/>
                <resource name='six.rb'/>
            </by-organisation>
            ...
```

Let's say that `seven.coffee` is a third party resource included within the software distribution. To illustrate how to add a third party resource sharing the primary license, let's say that `seven.coffee` is licensed under the primary (MIT) license from the Apache Software Foundation. After checking that details haven't already been added for `apache.org`, we add an `organisation` and [refer to it](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--why-ids) `by-organisation` containing the `resource`.

```text
    ...
    <organisations>
        ...
        <organisation
            id='apache.org'
            name='Apache Software Foundation'
            url='https://www.apache.org'/>
    </organisations>
    ...
    <within dir='alpha'>
        <with-license id='osi:mit'>
            <by-organisation id='example.org'>
                <resource name='five.js '/>
                <resource name='six.rb'/>
            </by-organisation>
            <by-organisation id='apache.org'>
                <resource name='seven.coffee'/>
            </by-organisation>
        </with-license>
    </within>
</manifest>
```

Finally, let's say that directory `beta` contains only `eight.java`, again from `apache.org` but licensed this time under the [Apache License, Version 2](https://opensource.org/licenses/apache-2.0), which is the standard license for Apache software.

After checking that this license isn't already in `licenses`, we add the license details and note the id [we chose](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--id-freedom) - in this case `osi:AL2`.

```text
    ...
    <licenses>
        ...
        <license
            name='Apache License, Version 2'
            id='osi:AL2'
            url='http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0.txt'>
<text>
                                 Apache License
                           Version 2.0, January 2004
                        http://www.apache.org/licenses/

      "License" shall mean the terms and conditions for use, reproduction,
      and distribution as defined by Sections 1 through 9 of this document.

      the copyright owner that is granting the License.

      other entities that control, are controlled by, or are under common
```

The `beta` directory in our distribution contains only the resource `eight.java` by `apache.org` licensed under the Apache License, Version 2. For this example, we will assume that no NOTICE is [required](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-faq--when-notice-needed). So we're ready now to complete the meta-data.

```text
    ...
    <within dir='beta'>
        <with-license id='osi:AL2'>
            <by-organisation id='apache.org'>
                <resource name='eight.java'/>
            </by-organisation>
        </with-license>
    </within>
</manifest>
```

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-examples-in-5-mins-index--And_Finally"></a>

We end up with this meta-data ([open](./in-5-mins-sample.xml) as an annotated sample):

```xml
OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE,  ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION
```

# apache-whisker:help – Apache Whisker::Plugin4Maven

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-help-mojo--apache-whisker.3Ahelp"></a>

# apache-whisker:help

**Full name**:

org.apache.creadur.whisker:apache-whisker-maven-plugin:0.2-SNAPSHOT:help

**Description**:

Display help information on apache-whisker-maven-plugin.
  
Call `mvn apache-whisker:help -Ddetail=true -Dgoal=<goal-name>` to display parameter details.

**Attributes**:

- The goal is thread-safe and supports parallel builds.

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-help-mojo--Optional_Parameters"></a>

## Optional Parameters

| Name | Type | Since | Description |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <detail> | boolean | - | Iftrue, display all settable properties for each goal.Default:falseUser Property:detail |
| <goal> | String | - | The name of the goal for which to show help. If unspecified, all goals will be displayed.User Property:goal |
| <indentSize> | int | - | The number of spaces per indentation level, should be positive.Default:2User Property:indentSize |
| <lineLength> | int | - | The maximum length of a display line, should be positive.Default:80User Property:lineLength |

<a id="creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-jdepend-report--explanations"></a>

# Explanation

[ [summary](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-jdepend-report--summary) ] [ [packages](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-jdepend-report--packages) ] [ [cycles](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-jdepend-report--cycles) ] [ [explanations](#creadur-apache-org-whisker-apache-whisker-maven-plugin-jdepend-report--explanations) ]   
  
The following explanations are for quick reference and are lifted directly from the original JDepend documentation.  
  
Explanation
Term
Description
Number of Classes
The number of concrete and abstract classes (and interfaces) in the package is an indicator of the extensibility of the package.
Afferent Couplings
The number of other packages that depend upon classes within the package is an indicator of the package's responsibility.
Efferent Couplings
The number of other packages that the classes in the package depend upon is an indicator of the package's independence.
Abstractness
The ratio of the number of abstract classes (and interfaces) in the analyzed package to the total number of classes in the analyzed package. The range for this metric is 0 to 1, with A=0 indicating a completely concrete package and A=1 indicating a completely abstract package.
Instability
The ratio of efferent coupling (Ce) to total coupling (Ce / (Ce + Ca)). This metric is an indicator of the package's resilience to change. The range for this metric is 0 to 1, with I=0 indicating a completely stable package and I=1 indicating a completely instable package.
Distance
The perpendicular distance of a package from the idealized line A + I = 1. This metric is an indicator of the package's balance between abstractness and stability. A package squarely on the main sequence is optimally balanced with respect to its abstractness and stability. Ideal packages are either completely abstract and stable (x=0, y=1) or completely concrete and instable (x=1, y=0). The range for this metric is 0 to 1, with D=0 indicating a package that is coincident with the main sequence and D=1 indicating a package that is as far from the main sequence as possible.
Cycles
Packages participating in a package dependency cycle are in a deadly embrace with respect to reusability and their release cycle. Package dependency cycles can be easily identified by reviewing the textual reports of dependency cycles. Once these dependency cycles have been identified with JDepend, they can be broken by employing various object-oriented techniques.
